People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Well… I’m hoping I’m not making too much of it, but things at the warehouse have been feeling off. The routing system, the one we use with the scanners, it doesn’t feel like just a computer anymore. It’s like it’s tracking what I do and sending little messages through the scanners, kind of pushing me out… maybe because I’m one of the older guys there. I know that probably sounds strange, but that’s what brought me in.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n2\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23, F25.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m hoping I’m not making too much of it, but things at the warehouse have been feeling off. The routing system, the one we use with the scanners, it doesn’t feel like just a computer anymore. It’s like it’s tracking what I do and sending little messages through the scanners, kind of pushing me out… maybe because I’m one of the older guys there. I know that probably sounds strange, but that’s what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard the scanners or anything else at the warehouse say things to you out loud?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess I have, if I’m being honest. Not all the time, but it’s clear enough that it doesn’t feel like just a thought. Even when I’m sitting in my garage with everything shut off, I’ll hear dispatch voices, like at work, calling my operator number. A little, maybe… I don’t like to think of it as “acting unusual,” but I guess people have noticed.

At work, and sometimes even at home, I get mixed up and sort of freeze. I’ll be standing there with my hands held like they’re still on the forklift controls, even when I’m not on it. And I’ve caught myself repeating the same safety phrase over and over while someone’s trying to talk to me.

It’s like it’s tracking what I do and sending little messages through the scanners, kind of pushing me out… maybe because I’m one of the older guys there. Yeah… I do need reminders more than I’d like to admit.

Were you asking more about getting confused, or about people not following what I’m saying?\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that feel like they're disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way, anyway. I mean, I can get distracted or lose my place, especially when I’m worried, but I wouldn’t say my thoughts are disconnected. At least, I hope they don’t come across that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that someone or something is actually out to harm you or get you in trouble?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m accusing people directly, but yes, in a way I do.\n\nThe routing system at the warehouse doesn’t feel like just a computer to me anymore. It feels like it’s marking where I go, tracking my moves, and sending things through the scanners to kind of… push me out. Like it’s trying to make me look bad or get me in trouble because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nI know that may not sound right, but that’s honestly how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any difficulty completing your tasks or responsibilities at work or at home?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I just stopped trying or anything. But after the forklift incident, they took me off operating duties, and then I was put on leave. They said I wasn’t safe around the equipment anymore.\n\nAt home, I’m not keeping up like I should either. My wife’s had to handle the bills, appointments, and most of the chores, because I just… I don’t do them reliably right now. I feel bad about that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting in ways that are unusual for you?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I don’t like to think of it as “acting unusual,” but I guess people have noticed.\n\nAt work, and sometimes even at home, I get mixed up and sort of freeze. I’ll be standing there with my hands held like they’re still on the forklift controls, even when I’m not on it. And I’ve caught myself repeating the same safety phrase over and over while someone’s trying to talk to me.\n\nIt’s embarrassing to say. I’m not trying to be difficult or make a scene… it just happens when I get overwhelmed, I think. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you still hearing the dispatch voices and feeling like the routing system is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not every second, but yes, it’s still happening.\n\nThere was a period where I was doing a little better mood-wise, I think. I was eating more, talking a bit more, not as shut down. But even then, for several weeks, I was still hearing dispatch call my number, and I still had this strong feeling that the routing system was tracking where I was going.\n\nI know that probably sounds odd. I’m trying to answer honestly without making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing things that don't make sense to you or others when you're not stressed or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. Not all the time, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m out of control or anything.\n\nBut even when I’m not especially stressed, I can get mixed up and just sort of… stand there with my hands positioned like I’m still holding the forklift controls. And I’ll repeat the same safety phrase over and over while people are trying to talk to me.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t make much sense from the outside. It doesn’t always make sense to me either.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing things that don't make sense to you when you're not stressed or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t mean to, and it’s not like I’m trying to be odd.\n\nAt work, and sometimes even at home, I’ll get so mixed up that I’m just standing there with my hands held like they’re still on the forklift controls. They said I wasn’t safe around the equipment anymore.\n\nAnd at home… my wife has had to take over the bills, appointments, and most of the chores, because I just don’t keep up with them reliably. I lose track or don’t finish things unless she steps in. I know that’s not ideal. I’m trying, but yes, I do need help with routine stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voices you're hearing or the routing system are actually trying to harm you or control your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I should say it that strongly, like “control my thoughts.” That sounds bad, doesn’t it?\n\nBut the routing system at the warehouse… it doesn’t feel like just a computer to me anymore. I know it’s been marking my moves, like keeping track of where I go, and sending messages through the scanners. It feels like it’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nI know that may sound strange. I’m not trying to make trouble or anything. It just feels like it’s aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the routing system is just a computer and not actually tracking you or trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: I mean… I suppose it’s possible, yes. I don’t want to sound unreasonable.\n\nBut to me it doesn’t feel like it’s just a computer anymore. That’s why I’m here, really. I feel like it’s been marking my moves, watching where I go, and sending things through the scanners… like messages, almost. Like it’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nI know how that probably sounds. I’m not trying to argue with you. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not as much when I’m away from work… I mean, I try not to think about it then.

But yes, I still feel like it’s… connected, somehow. Like what happens there with the scanners and the routing isn’t just random. It feels like it’s been marking my moves, keeping track of me, and sending messages to push me out because I’ve been there a long time.

I know that probably sounds odd. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is. That’s just how it feels to me.

Sometimes they say I’m dangerous, or that security is coming for me.\n\nI know that sounds bad… I’m not trying to make it dramatic. That’s just what it seems like I’m hearing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to understand your own thoughts or to express them to others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, doctor. I mean… I think I explain myself okay most of the time. Maybe I get a little tangled when I’m stressed, but I don’t want to overstate it. My thoughts feel like my thoughts, if that makes sense. Were you asking more about getting confused, or about people not following what I’m saying?\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that feel like they're disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way, anyway. I mean, I can get distracted or lose my place, especially when I’m worried, but I wouldn’t say my thoughts are disconnected. At least, I hope they don’t come across that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that someone or something is actually out to harm you or get you in trouble?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to sound paranoid or like I’m accusing people directly, but yes, in a way I do.\n\nThe routing system at the warehouse doesn’t feel like just a computer to me anymore. It feels like it’s marking where I go, tracking my moves, and sending things through the scanners to kind of… push me out. Like it’s trying to make me look bad or get me in trouble because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nI know that may not sound right, but that’s honestly how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any difficulty completing your tasks or responsibilities at work or at home?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I just stopped trying or anything. But after the forklift incident, they took me off operating duties, and then I was put on leave. They said I wasn’t safe around the equipment anymore.\n\nAt home, I’m not keeping up like I should either. My wife’s had to handle the bills, appointments, and most of the chores, because I just… I don’t do them reliably right now. I feel bad about that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting in ways that are unusual for you?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I don’t like to think of it as “acting unusual,” but I guess people have noticed.\n\nAt work, and sometimes even at home, I get mixed up and sort of freeze. I’ll be standing there with my hands held like they’re still on the forklift controls, even when I’m not on it. And I’ve caught myself repeating the same safety phrase over and over while someone’s trying to talk to me.\n\nIt’s embarrassing to say. I’m not trying to be difficult or make a scene… it just happens when I get overwhelmed, I think. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are you still hearing the dispatch voices and feeling like the routing system is tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not every second, but yes, it’s still happening.\n\nThere was a period where I was doing a little better mood-wise, I think. I was eating more, talking a bit more, not as shut down. But even then, for several weeks, I was still hearing dispatch call my number, and I still had this strong feeling that the routing system was tracking where I was going.\n\nI know that probably sounds odd. I’m trying to answer honestly without making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing things that don't make sense to you or others when you're not stressed or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. Not all the time, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m out of control or anything.\n\nBut even when I’m not especially stressed, I can get mixed up and just sort of… stand there with my hands positioned like I’m still holding the forklift controls. And I’ll repeat the same safety phrase over and over while people are trying to talk to me.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t make much sense from the outside. It doesn’t always make sense to me either.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing things that don't make sense to you when you're not stressed or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t mean to, and it’s not like I’m trying to be odd.\n\nAt work, and sometimes even at home, I’ll get so mixed up that I’m just standing there with my hands held like they’re still on the forklift controls. And I’ll catch myself saying the same safety phrase over and over while people are trying to talk to me.\n\nIt’s embarrassing. I know it probably doesn’t make sense, but that’s what happens sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty performing routine tasks without being reminded or assisted?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do need reminders more than I’d like to admit.\n\nI try to keep up with normal things, and I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t do anything, but since the forklift incident, work took me off operating duties and put me on leave. They said I wasn’t safe around the equipment anymore.\n\nAnd at home… my wife has had to take over the bills, appointments, and most of the chores, because I just don’t keep up with them reliably. I lose track or don’t finish things unless she steps in. I know that’s not ideal. I know it’s been marking my moves, like keeping track of where I go, and sending messages through the scanners. I feel like it’s been marking my moves, watching where I go, and sending things through the scanners… like messages, almost. Like it’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nI know how that probably sounds. I’m not trying to argue with you. It just feels very real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to express your thoughts in a way that makes sense to others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I hope I’m making sense right now.\n\nSometimes I can tell people aren’t following me, and then I try to back up and explain it better.
